I'm embarrassed because I don't know what I would add to all the thorough analyses of our budgetary developments for years ago and for years to come, but still. Today we are talking about the budget in our discussions. In order to shed light on the whole situation, however, as Mrs Alenka Bratušek said, all our public finances must be taken into account. For comparison, we can take data from 2011, when we had €36 billion 172 million of total GDP. Of this, we have spent 16 billion 584 million in the public sector. Of this, the budget we are talking about today was EUR 9 billion 362 million. Every time I talk about public spending, I look very carefully, and I am sorry that the financial service in our presentations has not prepared a comprehensive review for us. Last year we gave 4 billion 941 million euros for pensions. Through the health department, which is the third pillar, we have given 2 billion 340 million euros. We also forget the municipal treasury, which is the fourth cash register – EUR 2 billion 97,000. We have created a billion €540 million of government deficit last year. I have said these numbers to make sure that we are aware of the importance of public spending discussions, and I will link them to some of the reasons and answer questions about how to get to the situation so that it will be easier for us. Budgets are cost-effective. It is right that they are cost-effective. I hope that the proponents have been fair and have fairly distributed this savings into all segments of our consumers. I don't see any significant deviations. When making budgets, even when they were very luxurious, they were never everyone's satisfaction, they always wanted as much cake as possible. I hope that this has been done in a relative and fair manner and therefore would not have gone into the budget content for 2013 and 2014. However, from previous figures, I would like to go to another area that seems very important to me and that has a vital impact on our lives today. In 2007, we had plus 90 million in finance. In 2008, there was a deficit of €104 million in public finances, €980 million in 2009 and €896 million in 2010. A little less in 2011, as I said before. It seems a little funny that I'm talking about these numbers, but since we didn't realize in 2009 that our gross domestic product had fallen, and because we said to invest this money I don't know where, we didn't react and reduce public spending to an appropriate level of income and we produced a 2-billion-dollar hole and accumulated about 8 billion new debt – because we brought this deficit through debt, 8 billion euros, at current prices, as we are paying the loan, it's EUR 480 million. €480 million is 7% of the budget we are talking about. If we did not then owe ourselves and tighten the belt when this was necessary when we did not have enough income, now we would not have to pay this EUR 400 million interest for this money and this capital would not have to be returned! From this point of view, it's huge. When we were in debt and had such a large public deficit, there was an increase in the level of indebtedness. We went to the cost of interest with 300, 400, 500 – this year we will have 681 in the 2013 budget. So far, around 680 interest will be spent this year. You know, these are such huge amounts that I don't think are necessary. Without austerity measures, public services could be carried out if they started to save in time. We haven't done that, and now we're dealing with these problems. My only problem with the 2013 and 2014 budgets is that you still have 1.09 billion deficits in 2013 and 900 million euros in 2014. This will mean automatic again next year, without doing anything, an additional 60 million, which will have to be saved somewhere and will hurt us again for next year. I don't know why we can't put this in our heads and say that we don't need to know what kind of words and I don't know what kind of big politics and big conversations. The fact is, it has to be done. The only reason we have a problem with this budget is because it's too generous. Let's start spending when we have, then there won't be a problem. But we need to know that before we have to avoid the cost of high interest, we will have to return the principals. We live in advance. So it really takes sobriety. I've listened to so many and so many different comments that my head hurts. The next thing that I think is essential – the fact is that we had 37 billion 280 million GDP a few years ago. For 2013, GDP is expected to be 35 billion 800 million. I mean, we're expecting a cut. If we could keep employees, keep businesses, achieve a level of competitiveness so that our companies could obtain orders abroad so that they could create, then they could be Switzerland. Just because we don't want to get along, we don't want to listen, we don't do anything. One percent of GDP growth, which is 36 billion, is 360 million euros. Do you know how much money that is? How difficult it is to save, with all these taxes – there will be 10 million euros, there will be 15 million euros. You don't get to 360 million euros for the devil! It is essential that we focus and focus on how to achieve a lean country, increase our GDP, increase employment, increase the competitiveness of our economy. I have said many times that we can do this only to improve the conditions. I read in the paper that Mr. Akrapovich would like to build on Barja, and the bureaucrats say that because of the wild beasts and colossalists they will not allow him to do so. This can only happen in our country, where bureaucracy and stupidity rule smart and business people. I only made that point as an example. I would like to invite everyone once again to withdraw these referendum initiatives, which are hurting us, for God's sake, do stupid things! If they're not these banks, they're funding the economy, and we're putting down and sitting down. We're sitting in this government, state property, someone said. We're making a fool out of everything! We're making a fool of ourselves and supporting all the thievery in this country that's going on! Once again, the Civil Code will support the draft laws. We hope that the deficits will be smaller in realisation than we wrote them. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2012-12-05-SDZ6-Izredna-23.ana.u20	Thank you very much for your words, Madam Vice President. Dear Minister, distinguished colleagues and colleagues! The adoption of the budget for the next two years, in addition to some key reforms, is the most important moment in this mandate. It also includes the framework for future economic policy led by the state. Let's take a look at the state we're in. Slovenia, as a Central European small economy, is strongly involved in international financial and economic flows. That's what we wanted. We have entered the European Community and the common monetary union, we have taken over the euro's common currency. What does that mean? Let us now leave legislative obligations to Europe. This means that we have committed ourselves to the fall of physical borders, that we have accepted the game played by the most advanced economies. Suddenly we need to be competitive, suddenly we have to compete in global markets. Suddenly, our country contests, which we sometimes left far behind – Slovakia, Poland, the Czech Republic, and Hungary, are overcoming us. With such a dynamic that we have in recent years, in a few years, we will change from the country that the European Union has set as a model to the model example of imported state economic policies. True, the global crisis was expected unprepared, virtually without the euro on the bad weather reserve. But they followed 3 years of blindness and 3 years of lying to themselves, it's not so bad that it's somehow going to work itself out. He didn't. The huge debt that we pay high interest for today did not help. Mostly because of the fact that the borrowed money did not go into investment, but rather into the pursuit of economic growth, but into the artificial maintenance of the standard of living of the population, at least those who lost jobs in the real sector as per the current lane. Today, it is not enough that we are better than the former countries of the common republic, today we must be the best in Europe and not in the Balkans. That was the goal we set ourselves. Slovenia has already promised in 2009 how we will make arrangements to get our finances in order, so that there is no problem. The public budget deficit was initially 6%, 5.7% next year and 6.4% next year. For the fourth year, we have been in breach of the Stability and Growth Pact. It is true that other countries have violated it, but all other offenders, with the exception of Greece, Portugal and Cyprus, now have public finances under control, within the framework of what is allowed, because they have reformed and invested in a less burdened business environment. What about Slovenia? We're arguing about saving, and saving hasn't even started yet. We are arguing about a wrong neoliberal policy, and we have not yet smelled liberalism. We're arguing about special pensions, and soon we won't even be able to pay regulars anymore. If I look at a few cases that are confused by basic logic and, in truth, love, there is no European who would understand it – a means for education. Does anyone today hope to expose, step forward and say that Slovenia's secondary and higher education system is better than it was decades ago? Is it really just a lack of money, or is it possible that the rabbit is involved in another bush? We clearly do not distinguish more than between investing in knowledge that will achieve added value and investing in knowledge only on the budget line. Either at the same time we can't work anymore or there may be a problem that we have the most enrolled students per capita, but at the same time a very low proportion of those who are also graduating. That we are not even talking about any planned study policy in relation to the actual needs for staff in the country. Today we are educating young people to apply for employment. Internationally comparable indicators of the quality of the education system, with the exception of a few technical colleges that achieve excellent results, are steadily falling. Also general education and even literacy. Is that really why it's all about the lack of money? ParlaMint-SI_2012-12-05-SDZ6-Izredna-23.ana.u46	Thank you so much for saying that. Allow me to respond to a few key points or comments, comments that have been made on the draft budget, in particular in the context of the presentations on the views of the groups. First of all, I would like to say something about the obvious that the government has no economic policy. I think that the Government has a very clear economic policy, which is also thoughtful and works simultaneously in several areas. The first problem, the first situation we started dealing with at the beginning of the mandate, was the budget in force at the time, the projection of more than 6% of the deficit and the consequent breach of the country's commitments and the violation of the rules of the Stability and Growth Pact programme based on the established surplus deficit procedure against Slovenia. Therefore, for the first few months, as you know, practically the whole government devoted itself to preparing a rebalancing for this year. With this, we have reduced this year's deficit. I will talk about the various deficit assessments later, but the fact is that we have reduced it and the fact is that this reduction is credible. Regardless of whether there is a minor difference in any number, everyone today sees it as a successful reduction in the deficit this year. We continue this policy and propose a slower pace with these budgets in the coming years. Reducing the deficit – if we went close to 3% this year, we are only going below 3% in the coming years due to the economic situation. We are slowing down the savings rate that was foreseen at the time when we were adopting the balance sheet, which we were also reporting at the time, due to the economic situation. This is part of economic policy. The second part of economic policy is the reforms we are adopting these days. Some were accepted or accepted by the National Assembly in September or the first week of October. It is a law on holding and a law on resolving the problem of bad banking claims. As you know, both laws are still required for a referendum, they are not yet established and cannot be implemented. Yesterday a pension reform was adopted, and labour market reform is likely to be adopted in a short time. These are important reforms. By what we did at the beginning of the year in the area of the budget and what we are continuing with this proposal, and also with reforms that are partly adopted, partly in some kind of blockade, in some delay, we have ensured that we can continue to finance our needs. If we did not do so, we would be in a situation today where we could not even plan such a deficit or be dictated by others to what extent we can. The second part of economic policy is economic measures. In the presentation of the budget, I talked about significantly higher investment expenditure, and I talked about incentives for economic activity in the tax area. These are important measures. Moreover, what I believe is crucial for the start-up of economic activity in the coming year is a successful solution to the problem of poor banking claims or balance-sheets and, in this context, over-indebtedness of the economy. We know where we are about that. The referendum requirement shall be considered in the Constitutional Court. This is crucial, without it there will be no end to the credit crunch, and without the end of the credit crunch there will be no opportunity for a major expansion of economic activity, no matter what happens in an international environment where companies could export. If you look at the economic situation, they indicate a drop in exports. This is certainly not the result of savings, but is due to the inability of companies to finance their business in banks, even when they have a market for it. The third element is attracting foreign investment. It consists of two components. One is the simplification of the economic environment. This is not so much the responsibility of the Ministry of Finance, so I will not tell you much more about this, but you know what measures have been taken in this summer package to promote the economy. The issue related to attracting foreign investment is the sale of State shares in some companies that are privately owned or not in the dominant ownership of a country for which, at least for some of them, there is an interest. You can monitor this on your own all the time, but here we wanted to make these procedures easier, simpler, more transparent. This law is now under some kind of referendum requirement. With regard to economic policy and its philosophy, I would like once again to point out that there can be no dilemma of consolidation or growth in Slovenia. Public financial consolidation, at least to the extent that we have carried out it this year and planned it over the next two years, is a condition that the state can finance its needs at all, that it can finance its budget and is a condition for obtaining some credibility, not only for those investors who invest money in government bonds, but also for those who invest money in businesses, because they also watch the country's credit rating. Even when the rating of banks and major economic debtors is assessed, the creditworthiness of the country in which these economic operators, in particular banks, operate, is an important element in the assessment of their creditworthiness. It is therefore impossible to imagine that a country that has too many deficits would not fulfil its obligations while having an economy that would easily finance and expand its activities. It goes with each other. When we talk about these international debates, it is very clear that countries with major public financial problems that do not do what is a general objective do not consolidate public finances, must do this task irrespective of the dilemma, whether public-financial savings have a short-term negative impact on economic growth or not, because with a little longer perspective this is the only option that exists. Countries that have already done such consolidation, such as Germany in recent years, or countries that have not had such major public financial problems at all – in these countries, the issue of savings or growth is much more relevant. As I said before, these budgets suggest that the pace of deficit reduction in the coming years is calming, including due to the dilemma between fiscal consolidation, public finance balance on the one hand and economic growth on the other. As far as the various deficit assessments are concerned, I would like in particular to clarify a little more the different methodologies that exist. In this country, budgets are proposed, monitored, accepted and implemented on the basis of actual cash flows. When we talk about the deficits of the central state, including with both cash registers, when we adopt budgets, we are talking about this deficit. When I say that we are planning a deficit of 2.8% of gross domestic product next year, I am talking about this deficit. In the European Union, in particular by monitoring the reduction of the objectives under the Stability Programmes, a methodology based on the accounting principle shall be applied on the principle of commitments undertaken both by the budget and by those who pay in the budget. In this area, an informal assessment was made in the autumn. This assessment is based on data from the Ministry of Finance and published by the Statistical Office as well as the European Statistical Office. This assessment at the time referred to an indicative deficit of 4.2%. That's what we heard today. This was then an assessment based on movements in the first two quarters of the year or the first two thirds of the year. I said earlier that the budget balance this year, as far as income and expenditure are concerned, will not be necessary. What's open is the question of recapitalisation. The big question is whether the loan we gave to the New Ljubljana Bank this year will be included in the deficit or next year or never, as we understand the methodology in this area. This will not be decided by the Ministry of Finance, but by our Bureau of Statistics and Europe, because fortunately we do not have a system in which the Minister of Finance would make his own balance sheets to assess whether he has achieved his objectives or not. This is what independent institutions do. When there will be a deficit due to the loan to Nova Ljubljanska Bank in the form of a purchase of conditionally convertible bonds, it depends on when and if there will be any change in these bonds to capital. I have no reason to believe that this will happen this year, which means it is very likely that this exchange will happen next year. However, if there is any other solution to the bank's recapitalisation in the meantime, this deficit may not happen at all because there will be no final gain of the bank's share. As far as this year is concerned, we have talked about this when we adopt the rebalancing, and this has also been stated that after this the so-called ESA methodology will be followed by a system of European deficit accounts around 3.5% of gross domestic product this year. The estimate of about 4% is probably the most realistic at this point, and this is about the one that was made informally in the summer. According to one structure, this is likely to be quite different at the end of the year. Officially, this information will be known, as some of you know, in April next year when the competent institutes publish it. As far as next year is concerned, our aim is to reduce the gap between the cash flow deficit and those arising in accordance with the accounting principle, mainly because, with the exception of the resolution of the banking sector, these recapitalisations will be smaller or virtually non-existent and fewer cases will increase this difference. I think next year we will be able to narrow the gap between the monetary deficit of the budget, if I say so, and the accounting deficit, which means that we will also bring it closer to 3%. As far as the question is concerned, whether what we have proposed regarding the recapitalisation of banks is a one-off solution or not. I have repeatedly said that it is difficult to talk about the recapitalisation of banks being a one-off solution if we do this every year. Just because this was done every year and because there were no obvious results, we proposed a different approach. This approach means, simplifiedly said, that we are trying to recognise the whole problem in banks at once and suddenly solve it, with an intervention. We've never denied it. That is why we have also written and proposed a special law for this measure. This measure means that the State will once again definitively determine the extent of the problem of bad claims in banks and address them through one single guarantee measure via a specific company and solve them outside banks. This quickly leads us to banks that have cleared balance sheets that can increase lending activity. On the other hand, we get a mechanism to deal with corporate over-indebtedness. If this law is enforced in the second year and we issue these guarantees, this will prove to be a significant increase in the deficit in one year. This possible increase in deficit is already included in the debt growth projections previously presented. We are talking about about about 10% of gross domestic product, but we always say that these claims will also be cashed, that insurance will be cashed and that state shares in banks will be sold. All of these are also by law, which the National Assembly has already adopted, possible or envisaged sources of financing for the resolution of bad bank claims. There are many reasons, I have been talking about this for a long time, that we can say that in the end, when we draw the line in five or more years, practically all the debt that will be invested in bank recovery will be repaid. Once again, I would like to say that this has now come back to light in the context of the budgetary debate that the way we have proposed has been chosen to resolve bank claims, more expensive. This is not due to the fact that the only difference between the way we proposed and the way someone else proposed or advocated is whether we save bad bank claims outside banks, at some special institution, or whether we save them in banks. If we decide on one or the other approach, there are no bad claims, no more, no less, just as many as there are. If banks formed their domestic bad banks – by the way, the law that is now in the referendum allows and enforces this possibility, and may not be established – and bear bad claims on them, the state would probably give a guarantee for these bad claims, otherwise it has not done anything. If I were to give a guarantee for these bad claims, then I should give them just as much as she will now give to the bank management company, because, as it is said, the bad claims are, as far as they are, whether they are in banks or are being transferred from a bank. Once we decide that the state will provide guarantees for bad bank claims in order to clean up bank finances, these guarantees are the same regardless of how we decide. But if we don't decide to give these guarantees, then we can continue to give capital to banks. Some of this will be necessary in these operations, but less. If we were to put only capital into banks now, banks would write off these bad claims this year and next year and in two years and three years, and eventually we should put as much capital into the bank as we will now guarantee these bad claims. Recapitalisations that would have been required without a guarantee for poor claims without shielding bad claims in order to be excluded from normal bank balances either in banks or outside banks would have been required at the end, as much as the bad claims would have been written off. Poor claims will be written off as many as there are, regardless of whether we recapitalise or transfer them. Finally, there is a capital cost of claims that will be written off, which will not be cashed in the current value. Either we cover it with recapitalisations, direct ones, or guarantees — it costs the same thing because the problem is just as big. It seemed to us, and I still think it is very wise, that it is better to give guarantees that the state can repay by selling this property than to put capital in some institutions where the state, although the owner, does not have a direct chance to manage them. I think it is right that the Minister of Finance is not, in addition to being the Minister of Finance, the Director of the Bank for the Surface. But that's why it doesn't make sense that he owns this bank. The third issue that has been discussed, and this is the last thing I want to say about it, is the issue of a fiscal rule. We have made new simulations based on recent economic results or forecasts that have worsened, as we know. The question is whether we are acting in accordance with the fiscal rule or not. Let me first tell you what these predictions showed. As you know, because we have often explained this when we proposed a fiscal rule, the fiscal rule is designed to allow a deficit when the economic situation is poor. Due to these deteriorating forecasts, not only for this year and for the next year, but also after a slower recovery in the coming years, which is foreseen by the various forecasts of the Office for Macroeconomic Analysis and Development, it was estimated that a deficit of more than 300 million public finances would be allowed in 2015. When we talked about this in the summer, I think this number was significantly smaller. I'm not going to say what kind, but a much smaller one. Almost 330 million, as I have written here, is almost 1% of the gross domestic product. Even if a fiscal rule were adopted in the Constitution, as we have proposed, in 2015, due to poor or not excellent economic conditions, slow recovery, we could still have a 1 % deficit in public finances. If we adopted the fiscal rule as we suggested – I am not saying that it is the only way to accept it as we have proposed, but I am talking about what we have proposed. At that time, it was suggested that, for the first time in 2015, we should draw up a budget in order to respect the fiscal rule. I think we will all agree that from here where we are today, it is not too difficult to get to 1% of the gross domestic product of the deficit in 2015. What we have yet proposed to be enshrined in the Constitution is that in the period until 2015 when the fiscal rule enters into force, the Government should propose budgets by drawing closer to that objective. That's exactly what we suggested. Let me just say two numbers. For 2012, we found that if we already had a fiscal rule and we should fully respect it, then the deficit in 2012, after what was proposed in the initial budget, should have been – we would have had almost a billion differences. We are now reducing this difference to half with the proposals of this year's rebalancing and the budget proposed for next year. In 2015, I think that additional measures that would not be too difficult could lead to compliance with the fiscal rule – a deficit of approximately 1% of gross domestic product. That's what I was talking about. This year we are going with a budget slightly below 3%, and next year even more, and then in a significantly better economic situation than today's 2015, we would have made this additional effort and would have been around where the fiscal rule would have ordered it. So there is no big difference between what we have proposed as a fiscal rule and what we are now proposing as a draft budget. Thank you very much.

I have tabled an amendment to the 2013 budget relating to the maintenance of the indemnified portion of the Idri mercury mine, the monitoring of closures and mining damage. This will not save the Slovenian economy, nor will it bury the budget, far from that, but this is an opportunity for a very promising development project. In the event of the destruction of the mine, we can get a very interesting development project. The mine in Idrija was the second largest silver mine in the world. This year, along with the largest mine in Almaden, Spain, they joined the prestigious UNESCO World Heritage List. In Idrija, between 6 and 10% of the world's mercury reserves still exist. When the mine closed down or when a decision was made to shut down gradually in the mid - 70 ’ s, the price was $90. For ecological reasons, the production and use of mercury have been abandoned. Although today the price of a cylinder on the open market is $2,000, there will be no more production and use in the European Union. Today it is used only in Mongolia, China, South America, for example, Brazil, etc. In 2005, the European Union adopted a Community strategy on mercury, which prohibited the use and trade in mercury from 2000 onwards. Since 2009, activities have been under way within the UN to conclude the UN World Convention on Mercury, and the Government of the Republic of Slovenia has been actively involved. These documents are related to the opportunities Slovenia has, as we can exercise exceptional knowledge and experience about the impact of mercury on human health and the environment. At the end of 2009, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia adopted a decision on the liquidation of the Idria mercury mine. Since then, a number of activities have been under way to continue and regulate the legal obligation arising from the Law on the Prevention of the Effects of Mining in the Idrija Mercury Mine. After the destruction of the mine, care will still have to be taken to maintain the indemnified part of the mine, monitor the closures, repair the environment and mining damage, and ensure the management and maintenance of the cultural, natural and technical heritage associated with the Idrija mine. I said earlier that this heritage is on the UNESCO World Heritage List. On the basis of the Law on the Prevention of the Effects of Mining in the Mercury Mine, Idria will be implementing by the end of 2012, by the end of this year, a programme for the maintenance of the unspoiled part of the cave, monitoring and mining damage. This was a programme that started in 2008 and will be completed this year and adopted by the government in 2007. The mine has prepared a programme for the period 2013-2017 which is substantially sub-valued in the budgets for the next two years, 2013 and 2014. This item has remained, but this will not be sufficient for the full implementation of this programme, so I have also tabled an amendment. Mining damage is a legal category based on mining legislation. The mining damage is paid for all the damage caused by the thousands of years of mining on infrastructure and facilities in the city of Idrija. This item was also part of the 2012 budget and was then halved by a balance sheet and the Ministry of Finance measure stopped the implementation of the budget and the payment of mining damage. People saved the environment. For example, in a particular case in the city centre under the protection of Unesco, a group of tenants repaired a monumentally protected facility. They have invested their money, they have expected some of the mining damage, the thing has been restored, it has been done, and now they are no longer entitled to this money because the implementation of the state budget on this item has been suspended. Another thing that's connected to the opportunity I'm talking about. When it comes to the liquidation of the mine and this company, it is a matter of transforming this company into a public institution. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia adopted a decision last year as a condition for entering the list of World Heritage Sites at UNESCO and established the Center for the Management of the Heritage of Living Silver Idrija. This decision alone is not enough. This institution should now be put into operation. It's basically a transformation, not a new institution. It is not a new entity, but merely a matter of transforming the company into a public institution, as it will continue to be necessary to carry out all the legal obligations undertaken so far by the mine and to take care of the technical, natural and cultural heritage and to monitor the closures. This mine will never be completely closed, and it will have to continue to be maintained because it is located just under a city of 6,000 inhabitants, unless the state decides to build a new city and move these 6,000 people, which I do not believe in. Therefore, this institution will be able to care for what is legally required to be cared for. In this context, the previous government established the Live Silver Information Research Centre in 2008. In Idrija, we have a very clear new development project that can be created on the basis of the extraordinary heritage of a former world-renowned mine. I expect this government to take all measures to transform the company into a management centre as soon as possible, which was one of the conditions for entering the Unesca list. Otherwise, we may be threatened with deletion from this list, which would be a great disgrace to our country. In addition, I propose that the State Election adopt the amendment that I have tabled in the 2013 budget. Thank you very much.

In this case, there are also cases where contributions to pension and disability insurance have been paid. Therefore, with this amendment, we want to restore the constitutional right that pensioners had, and we are returning to the budget the funds that should have been provided for the payment of pensions to individuals who already belonged to them. ParlaMint-SI_2012-12-05-SDZ6-Izredna-23.ana.u266	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Dear Members and Members! Allow me to present briefly the Proposal for a Law on the Implementation of the Budgets of the Republic of Slovenia for 2013 and 2014. In addition to those things that are normally regulated by such laws and are necessary for us to be able to implement the budget at all — without such a legal basis, it is impossible to implement the budget, even if a number of parts of the budget were adopted — there are still some important provisions in this law that may not be normal or are not adopted each year, and I would like to draw particular attention to them. First, it is a restriction on the transfer of so-called assigned revenue between years of three twelfths, or then in 2014 of two twelfths of revenue, which run in one year. In doing so, we want to encourage the use of earmarked resources, not to be carried over to the next year, because earmarked funds are specific in the budget, under the Public Finance Act they should be as low as possible due to the principle of an integrated budget and we want them to be used on an ongoing basis for the purposes for which they are intended. The Budget Implementation Act regulates how the objectives of reducing costs, the mass of wage expenditure and other labour costs will be achieved, by authorising ministers and heads of public sector bodies or institutions to implement all necessary to implement their financial plans within the framework of the funds envisaged. In the case of direct budget users or ministries, this means that ministers will have to, and we have already begun, implement appropriate reorganisation measures to ensure that we do not spend more on salaries than is foreseen. In the field of institutions or other public sector where ministers do not have direct control, this means that they will have to provide a starting point for the development of financial plans to ensure that institutions remain within the framework of the resources provided for in the budget for transfers to institutions in specific areas and that such financial plans can be drawn up, they will also have to prepare appropriate adjustment measures in the area of wages or wages. Since the implementation of budgets, particularly in institutions or in the wider public sector, is always difficult to ensure compliance with its guidelines – this is what we have seen this year – let's say in the field of regression in some institutions, in the field of employment where we do not work in the public administration or practically not, while in the wider public sector, despite all budgetary constraints, we have proposed in this law provisions which impose an obligation on the heads of institutions and other similar institutions in the public sector to find that they will not be able to operate within the framework of the funds provided for in the budgets or transfers to institutions and their financial plans, they shall draw up an appropriate recovery plan and submit it within the time limit laid down by law. They are personally responsible for this, both by their position and by the fine provided for in the law. The law also provides that, by the time all these measures are implemented, it is not possible to pay work performance in order to ensure that such savings and incentives are made as soon as possible. The last thing that matters is a provision that allows the government or gives the possibility of transferring additional funds from the capital company to the budget if the contributions and funds provided for in the budget are not able to provide sufficient funds for the payment of pensions. In particular, it is a security measure if, for example, the effects of pension reform were not achieved by those envisaged for the following year. At the same time, it was set out in the Proposal for a Holding Act that 10% of the potential purchases of State property would be transferred to Kad by maintaining the long-term stability of this company. Thank you.
ParlaMint-SI_2012-12-05-SDZ6-Izredna-23.ana.u267	Thank you very much. The draft law was treated by the Committee on Finance and Monetary Policy as a parent labour force. For the presentation of the report of the Committee, I give the floor to the member, Mag. Mark Pogačnik.
ParlaMint-SI_2012-12-05-SDZ6-Izredna-23.ana.u268	Madam President, thank you for the floor. At its 39th emergency meeting, on 1 December, the Finance and Monetary Policy Committee considered the draft law on the implementation of the 2013 and 2014 budgets of the Republic of Slovenia submitted to the National Assembly for consideration by the Government of the Republic of Slovenia. The Committee adopted the following additional material: opinions of the Legislative and Legal Service, position of the interested group of non-economic activities of the State Council of 9 November. In addition, the Committee received comments or proposals for amendments from various institutions and legal entities concerned. Representatives and representatives of the Ministry of Finance as representatives of the proposer, a representative of the Legislative and Legal Service, a representative of the Confederation of Public Sector Trade Unions and a representative of the Pergam Trade Unions Confederation. In addition, the State Secretary of the Ministry of Finance said that the explanation of the articles was a law allowing the implementation of budgets for those years. At the same time, he stressed that the savings-oriented text of the law, at the same time as the most urgent measures to increase the revenue of the State budget, namely the need to reduce the expenditure of budgets at all levels, sets out the composition of the specific budget implementation, the extent of the country's debt and guarantee, the amount of the average amount belonging to the municipalities and the way in which the commitments are made. He pointed out that the substantive provisions of the law itself are the costs of the current regime. The bill requires careful monitoring of expenditure from the budget while being monitored. In the debate, the opposition parties presented their views and opinions, expressed their concerns and raised certain issues, as was the case with regard to the financing of discharges, labour costs during the notice period and unemployment benefits during the first three months following the termination of the employment relationship for business reasons, where, in the opinion expressed, sufficient budgetary provision or special budget item should be drawn up. The Committee was also presented with the views and opinions of the representatives of the trade unions present, from which they generally do not want to be amended by technical law and other law. The debate also opened with a series of arrangements where representatives and representatives of the Government provided additional explanations to the Committee. In addition, the representative of the Government explained in a series of answers to questions relating to the relevance of the proposal as a whole, among other things, that the ZUJF regulates, in a particular part, the right to work performance for employees in the public sector by regulating the possibility or restricting the payment of work performance according to the available resources of the individual budgetary user. In the manner described, an arrangement may be likened to an arrangement in respect of jubilee awards carried out on the basis of an agreement with the public sector trade unions, due to the repeated misinterpretation of such a provision of the current ZUJF in practice. A range of viable options were presented for reducing labour costs, from reorganisation of work, more economical overpayments and work performance to retirement, both after an express assessment, the introduction of forgiveness and the only viable options for reducing costs – not in one place. In conclusion, it was stressed that budget preparation is carried out consistently at the inter-ministerial level by discussing where and where funds are needed, where rationalisation is possible or where funds can be reduced. At the hearing, the Committee adopted amendments to coalition parliamentary groups and the Committee did not accept amendments from opposition parliamentary groups. In accordance with Article 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee then voted on and adopted all the articles of the draft law together. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the amended law in which the amendments adopted are incorporated is prepared under the first paragraph of Article 133 of the Rules of Procedure of the General Assembly. The amended bill is an integral part of this report.

ParlaMint-SI_2012-12-05-SDZ6-Izredna-23.ana.u270	Thank you very much, Madam Vice President. Dear colleagues and colleagues! Public debt combines the state's ability to function and places a burden on future generations. The fundamental principle of sustainable budgetary policy must be the cost-effective use of budgetary resources. Instead of looking for new and new tax resources that would further burden poorer segments of the population, we need to work to limit public expenditure at this time. In New Slovenia, we are aware that we cannot spend more than we create, so public finances need to be consolidated by reducing public spending. Under normal economic conditions, all national expenditure to finance public expenditure under ESA 95 may be up to 45 % of gross domestic product. The solution is a basis for a more predictable situation for entrepreneurship and signals to the public sector that it will share in good and bad the fate of the economy. The implementation of this law is an urgent and direct condition for the implementation of the budget and thus for its implementation. The basic objective of the law is to lay the legal basis for solutions that will enable the current and smooth implementation of the adopted budget. The Law on the implementation of the budget of the Republic of Slovenia follows to a large extent the principles and solutions that are enshrined in the Public Finance Act, by heavily limiting individual expenditure, by setting out the extent of public sector debt at national level in a given year and by defining permitted guarantees, also defines the additional powers of the Government. For faster and more efficient use of European funds and the elimination of liquidity problems for operators, the level of prepayments from 20% to 30% is raised. Prepayments are now also allowed for public funds and recipients of private and public law exercising public powers or public service under the law governing social security. This solution seems extremely important to us in New Slovenia. Representatives of direct budgetary users shall be obliged to ensure that labour costs are reduced by not exceeding the total annual level of resources. For 2013 and 2014, public servants and officials shall not be paid part of their salary for performance. The RTV contribution to natural and legal persons shall be reduced. The higher value real estate tax shall be applied for 2013 and 2014 where the property belongs to the same owner and their total value shall be at least EUR 500 000, which shall not apply to commercial and industrial property and property for public use. The proposal for a law on the implementation of the budget of the Republic of Slovenia also allows the Government to redistribute funds in substantial amounts, so I believe that the Government will find a solution for any reallocation of funds for the user by the Ministry of Education, Science, Culture and Sport. The New Slovenia Group supports the law. Thank you very much.
